### Third Part.

But God is the head of Christ. Likewise, that God has seated Christ at His right hand in heaven, above all principalities, power, might, dominion, and everything that may be named, not only in this world but also in the one to come. And He has appointed Him as the
<margin>Ephesians 1:21.</margin>
head of the congregation <note>original: "Gemeine," referring to the body of believers</note> over all things: which is a body, namely the fullness of Him who fills all in all. Regarding this, the royal prophet David already prophesied in the Old Testament when he spoke: You will make him Lord over the work of your hands: you have put all things under his feet; of which chapters 18 and 26 of this Third Part have treated more extensively. But when all things shall be thus subject to the Lord Christ, then shall be the end, and then he will deliver the Kingdom
<margin>Psalm 8:7.</margin>
<margin>1 Corinthians 15:28.</margin>
to God and his Father: and will abolish all authority, dominion, and power, and then the Son himself will also be subject to Him who put all things under him: so that God may be all in all. Therefore he says elsewhere, justly, rightly, and

<meta>This page is the title page of a new work following the conclusion of a theological "Third Part" previously translated. While the previous text dealt with the divine hierarchy and the end of Christ's kingdom, this new treatise by Julius Sperber focuses on historical wonders and the prophecy of a coming "Golden Age."</meta>

## A fine
# TREATISE
# OF JULIUS SPERBER,
## Concerning many wondrous, partly previously unheard of, or also unusual and strange things, events, and matters,

->Which occurred and happened not only generally before the end and after the beginning of every new major epoch <note>original: "Hauptzeit," a term often used in mystical or millennialist chronologies to denote a significant era of divine history</note>, but especially throughout these hundred years, from the year 1500 to the year 1600.<-

->Along with a summary and brief announcement of a time yet to come, being the final and golden age <note>original: "güldenen Zeit," referring to the millenarian concept of a future era of peace and enlightenment on Earth</note>, and its general circumstances.<-

->By<-

### BENEDICT BAHNSEN
->Brought to print.<-

The work was brought to press by Benedict Bahnsen, a significant figure in the preservation of mystical and Rosicrucian writings.</summary>

->**AMSTERDAM,**<-

<summary>This title page introduces Julius Sperber's treatise on the miraculous events of the 16th century and prophecies regarding the "final times." Published in Amsterdam in 1662, it was edited for print by Benedictus Figulus, a key figure in the dissemination of European mystical and alchemical literature.</summary>

<keywords>Julius Sperber, Benedictus Figulus, Amsterdam, 17th century publishing, prophecy, historical wonders, eschatology, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page follows the title page of the work by Julius Sperber, edited by Benedictus Figulus and published in Amsterdam in 1662. It begins the editor's preface, addressing the reader directly to explain the motivations behind publishing this decades-old manuscript.</meta>

<page-type>preface</page-type>
<sig>* ij</sig>
<meta>catchword: To (original: "Zum")</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative horizontal border at the top of the page, consisting of repeated stylized floral or leaf motifs (fleur-de-lis style).</image-desc>

# The Editor greets the Reader. <note>original: "Editor Lectori S.", an abbreviation for the Latin *Editor Lectori Salutem*, a standard greeting meaning "The editor sends greetings to the reader."</note>

Dear, friendly reader, why I have now undertaken to bring to light the present treatise—which was written more than forty years ago (since the highly enlightened author, **Julius Sperber**, is said to have departed this world <note>original: "diese Welt gesegnet haben soll," a common euphemism for death</note> in the year 1616)—is to be mentioned briefly: namely, to achieve, if possible, a three-fold purpose.

Firstly, because the great **GOD** continues to perform His miracles before the eyes of the world, and indeed increases them in various ways as an even greater testimony; so that the children of men may be given more cause to truly open their eyes, and so that God our Merciful One need not complain so much or so bitterly about the general worldly complacency <term>worldly complacency (Welt-sicherheit): a state of spiritual apathy where people feel falsely secure and ignore divine warnings</term> (wherein everyone busies themselves only with greed, gluttony, drunkenness, gambling, and luxury, but does not heed the works of the Lord nor look upon the deeds of His hands, **Isaiah 5:12**) and then be forced to strike us without further mercy and put an end to us <note>original: "den garauss spielen," a German idiom for finishing someone off or destroying them.</note>.

<summary>In this preface, the editor explains his reasons for publishing Julius Sperber's forty-year-old manuscript, primarily to wake a distracted society from its spiritual apathy by highlighting God's ongoing miracles.</summary>
<keywords>Julius Sperber, preface, miracles, spiritual apathy, Isaiah 5:12, divine judgment, 17th-century publishing</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues the editor's preface from the previous page, where he outlined his three-fold purpose for publishing Julius Sperber’s work. Having already explained the first reason (to wake people from spiritual apathy), he now details the second and third motivations. The initials B. B. at the end likely refer to Benedictus Bahnsen, a follower of the mystic Jacob Böhme and a frequent publisher of such works in Amsterdam.</meta>

Secondly, so that someone among the diligent **historians** <note>original Latin: *Historicis*</note> may be given cause to take better notice of such **great wonders of God’s miracles** <note>original Latin: *Magnalia prodigiorum DEI*</note>, and to diligently **collect** <note>original: *colligiren*</note> them from the time when the late **Sperber** <note>Julius Sperber (d. 1616) was a prominent counselor and mystical writer whose works circulated in manuscript form for decades before being printed</note> ceased until the present day, and to faithfully bring them to light for the common good (so as to awaken even more **admirers of God’s wonders** <note>original Latin: *Admirandorum Dei Miratores*</note>).

And then finally, so that I may again have the opportunity to plead with God-fearing hearts: if there should be any more of that dear man **Sperber's** writings available in **Silesia** <note>a historical region in Central Europe, home to many mystics of this period</note> or elsewhere (as is said to be the case), let them no longer sin against Christ’s teaching and commandment by covering a lit candle with a vessel or hiding it under a bench. Instead, they should place it upon a lampstand so that whoever enters may see the light, **Luke 8:16**. Whoever acts against this out of malice or for any other reason will bear their own judgment.

The rest may be read in the author’s own preface. **Amsterdam**, December 2, 1661.

->**B. B.**<-

<summary>The editor concludes his preface by calling on historians to document divine miracles and urging anyone holding unpublished manuscripts by Julius Sperber to release them for the public good, citing the biblical command to let one's light shine.</summary>
<keywords>Julius Sperber, miracles, Silesia, manuscript preservation, Luke 8:16, Benedictus Bahnsen, historians</keywords>

## Preface to the Reader.

Just as in all other things people are accustomed primarily and most of all to look upon physical and earthly matters / and to concern themselves with these: in the same way, the great majority of people in Christendom tend to concern themselves almost exclusively with transitory things regarding what is generally to be expected in the future: So much so / that if we take the currently impending **Hungarian war efforts** <note>This likely refers to the escalating tensions between the Habsburg Monarchy and the Ottoman Empire in the mid-17th century, culminating in the Austro-Turkish War of 1663–1664</note> as an example / everyone—spiritual and worldly persons, of high and low status—looks only at this: it is also / wherever people gather / the primary worry and topic of conversation: Whether the **Turk** <note>A standard 17th-century term for the Ottoman Empire and its military forces, often viewed as a "scourge" or a prophetic sign in European literature</note> will in time occupy this land still; or whether on the contrary we shall overcome him / and enrich ourselves from his lands / people and goods? Thus, everyone is far more concerned with obtaining physical freedom / than anyone should be concerned with how he might become free from the service of transitory existence / and to the glorious freedom of the children of Go-

<margin>Romans 8:21</margin>
->d<-

<summary>The author critiques the public's obsession with worldly politics and the Ottoman military threat in Hungary, arguing that people prioritize physical safety and wealth over the spiritual "freedom of the children of God."</summary>

<keywords>Hungarian War, Ottoman Empire, physical freedom, spiritual freedom, Romans 8, Christendom, worldly affairs</keywords>

<margin>Josephus, *The Jewish War*, Book 9, Chapter 31 <note>original: "De bell: Judaic. lib. 9. cap. 31." Flavius Josephus was a 1st-century Romano-Jewish historian whose accounts of the Jewish revolt were widely read in the 17th century as moral and prophetic warnings.</note></margin>

<meta>This page continues the preface from the previous page. The previous text described how the Jews before the destruction of Jerusalem were misled by signs and false interpretations of prophecy. # PREFACE.

But does it not happen exactly the same way among us Christians today? Whatever is prophesied for our benefit—serving a good outcome for us—that we believe steadfastly; but whatever is prophesied against us, that must be false and wrong. Consequently, the alleged prophecies being forged today are generally quite suspect and suspicious, and are almost always directed according to how each person is inclined <term>affectioniret</term> <note>original: "affectioniret." From the Latin *affectionatus*, meaning to be biased, influenced by emotion, or favorably disposed toward someone.</note> toward their religion or toward high-ranking persons. 

Thus, there is no one among the <term>Evangelicals</term> <note>In this period, "Evangelical" (Evangelisch) refers to Protestants, primarily Lutherans, following the Augsburg Confession.</note> who could have given any credit to the prophecies of certain <term>Papists</term> <note>original: "Bäpstler." A common, often polemical term used by Protestants to refer to Roman Catholics.</note> by which they wished to make King <term>Philip of Spain</term> <note>Philip II of Spain (1527–1598), whose vast global empire and defense of Catholicism led to Protestant fears of a "Universal Monarchy."</note> the future <term>Monarch</term> of the entire world; just as nothing eventually came of it. 

But when we Christians—both Papists and Evangelicals alike—hear or read prophecies of the downfall of the <term>Turk</term> <note>original: "Türcken." A general term for the Ottoman Empire, which was the primary military and religious rival to Christian Europe at the time.</note> and our victory against him, then every single person from both sides believes them steadfastly. In the same way, this prophecy—which the Turks themselves have regarding their own downfall and how they are to be entirely overcome by the Christians—is held by everyone as almost more significant than the proper prophetic revelations <note>The author uses "billiche Prophetische Weissagungen" to refer to "proper" or "legitimate" biblical prophecies, contrasting them with folk-prophecies or politically motivated predictions.</note> themselves.

<summary>The author critiques the confirmation bias of his contemporaries, noting that Christians only believe prophecies that favor their own religious or political factions. He highlights a rare point of agreement between Catholics and Protestants: the belief in the inevitable downfall of the Ottoman Empire.</summary>
<keywords>prophecy, confirmation bias, Evangelicals, Papists, Philip II of Spain, Ottoman Empire, religious conflict, 17th-century politics</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that contemporary Christians are as prone to self-deception regarding prophecy as the Jewish people were before the destruction of Jerusalem. The author shifts from historical parallels to the specific political and religious biases of his own time.</meta>

# PREFACE.

A ij

But is it not just the same among us Christians today? Whatever is prophesied for our benefit—that which serves our interests—we believe firmly; but whatever is prophesied against us, that must be false and wrong. Therefore, the supposed prophecies being forged in these days are commonly quite *suspect* <note>original: "suspect, verdächtig"</note> and doubtful, and are almost always directed according to how each person is *affectionate* <note>meaning favorably inclined or biased</note> toward a particular religion or toward high-ranking persons. For example, there is no one among the Evangelicals <note>Protestants, particularly Lutherans</note> who could have given any credit to the prophecies of certain Papists <note>Catholics</note> through which they wanted to make King *Philip* of *Spain* the future *Monarch* of the whole world; just as, in the end, nothing came of it. But when we Christians—both Papists and Evangelicals—hear or read prophecies about the downfall of the Turk <note>The Ottoman Empire, which was the primary external threat to Europe at this time</note> and our victory over him, then every single person on both sides believes them most firmly.

In the same way, this prophecy—which the Turks themselves have concerning their own downfall and how they shall be completely overcome by the Christians—is held by almost everyone to be of greater weight than the proper <note>meaning legitimate or biblical</note> Prophetic revelations.

<meta>catchword: For</meta>

<summary>The author critiques the selective nature of prophetic belief, arguing that people only believe predictions that suit their political or religious agendas. He highlights the common European obsession with the predicted collapse of the Ottoman Empire as a rare point of agreement between warring Christian factions.</summary>

<keywords>prophecy, religious bias, King Philip II of Spain, Ottoman Empire, Protestant-Catholic relations, political rhetoric, self-deception, 17th-century thought</keywords>

However, very few people are found
who pay attention to the universal change
that is so near at hand,
or who are accustomed to concern themselves
with that time.

Because Almighty
God knew all this well—how the complacent <note>original: "sichere," implying a state of false security or spiritual indifference</note> world
would pay no heed at all to the prophecies
of His prophets—He has arranged it with
special diligence so that every time
a change of the Great Ages <term>Haubtzeiten: literally "chief-times," referring to major dispensations or eras of human history</term> occurs,
certain signs and wonders must happen.
From these, everyone—even those
who otherwise refuse to believe
God's Word and the prophetic prophecies—
would be forced to conclude
beyond doubt that a transformation of the
universal Great Ages was at hand.
But in particular, God the Lord
has seriously commanded us in many places
of the Old and New Testaments to diligently
observe the certain signs that are to precede
the end of this current age and world.
Therefore, the Angel of the Lord also said
in a vision to the

# PREFACE.

precede such a beginning: and he <note>Likely referring to the prophet Ezra or the angel Uriel in the book of 2 Esdras</note> recounts twelve primary signs among them, which we have dealt with in detail in another place. From this it is now to be understood that the end of this present world and the beginning of the future time and glory can neither be calculated according to **arithmetic**, nor measured according to **geometry**, nor actually and certainly **predicted** according to **astrology**. Not to mention other subtle **calculations**, by which some presume to indicate a specific year for the end of this world. And accordingly, one cannot and should not know or deduce the certainty of such a time beforehand in any other way than solely from the wonders and signs which will occur and happen before and at the end of the current era of the New Testament, and also at the beginning of the future time and world. These signs and wonders are not merely common <term>signs: original Latin "Signa," meaning omens or indicators</term>, such as have occurred several times before in other eras; rather, they will be such miraculous signs that one will certainly have reason enough to be amazed by them (because they are unusual and have never before been heard, seen, or experienced in such a way). This is what the angel in the aforementioned place <note>A reference to 2 Esdras 9:1, "Measure thou the time diligently in itself"</note> calls measuring the time by itself:

<summary>The author argues that the end of the world cannot be predicted through mathematical or astrological calculations. Instead, one must look for unique, unprecedented miraculous signs as the only true indicators of the transition between eras.</summary>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous page, further defining the concept of "measuring the time by itself." It transitions from criticizing mathematical predictions to explaining the author's own method of historical and biblical comparison.</meta>

# PREFACE.

namely, according to the miraculous signs that are accustomed to occur beforehand and alongside them; not only at a single point in time, but in such a way that one measures one time against another: that is, that one compares the wonders and signs of one <term>primary era: original German "Haubtzeit," referring to the great epochs of world history</term> against the other primary eras and their *signs* <note>original Latin: "Signis"</note>: And afterward holds that same comparison up against the Holy Scripture. This is now such a prophecy which surpasses all **geometric prophecy**, **arithmetic**, and **astronomical calculation**, and even more so all far-fetched **conjectures** <note>original Latin: "Conjecturas"</note>; it also cannot fail or err. Thus, the heathens, Turks, Jews, and other unbelievers, being overcome by such infallible measurement, must give their approval and belief to it, **whether they wish to or not** <note>original Latin: "nolentes volentes"</note>. 

I have also undertaken such a measurement for myself. And I have established it not only from the Holy Divine Scripture (in which the godly surely place enough faith on its own), but for the most part also from impartial and proven histories, and well-known events that have occurred until now. Within this work, I maintain the following order: First, I deal with the division of the *centuries* <note>original Latin: "Seculorum," referring here to the great historical ages</note> or primary eras, and how these have been *distinguished* <note>original German: "distinguiret"</note> and from one another un-

<meta>catchword: ter- (completing "unterschieden" or "divided/distinguished")</meta>

All of these are such signs and wonders the likes of which have not easily been heard of or experienced before, or which have never otherwise occurred in such quantity and so frequently one after another. # Summary Contents
## of the Chapters
### Of this TREATISE.

I. From where one must learn the correct distinction of times.
II. How many <term>secula<note>original: "Secula"; Latin for ages or centuries.</note></term> or principal ages there are.
III. Of the first principal age or <term>century<note>original: "Seculo".</note></term>.
IV. Of the second principal age or century.
V. Of the third principal age or century.
VI. Whether this doctrine of the future golden age is in all respects a new and unheard-of doctrine?
VII. That one principal age is connected to another.
VIII. What existed and happened before the beginning of the first principal age.
IX. Of wondrous things that occurred after the beginning of the first principal age.
X. Of marvelous things that took place towards the end of the first principal age, and up to the beginning of the second principal age—that is, generally one after another during the hundred years before the birth of Christ.
XI. Of the earthquakes that were observed within the hundred years before the end of the first principal age, up to the beginning of the second principal age and thus until the birth of Christ.
XII. Of the powerful and manifold uprisings and rebellions of subjects against their <term>authorities<note>original: "Obrigkeit"; referring to the established government or ruling lords.</note></term> that occurred across the whole world during those same hundred years.

## Chapter One.

### From where one must learn the true distinction of the *Seculorum* <note>Latin: "ages" or "eras"</note> or times.

<image-desc>Large decorative drop cap 'G' with leafy ornamentation and a human face integrated into the letter's design.</image-desc>
Almighty God, when he created heaven and earth, also ordained at the same time not only days and hours, but also the *Secula* <note>Latin: ages or eras</note> or times, how they follow one after another; also when they should take their beginning and end. And he himself knows best (as Sirach <note>Referring to the Book of Sirach, or Ecclesiasticus, a book of the Apocrypha</note> says) at what time each thing shall happen.
<margin>
Sirach 43:19–20.
</margin>
He proclaims both that which is past and that which is to come, and reveals what is hidden. And the Prophet Daniel says: God changes 
<margin>
Daniel 2:21–22.
</margin>
time and hour. He deposes kings and sets up kings. He gives the wise their wisdom, and those of understanding their intellect. He reveals what is deep and hidden. He knows what lies in darkness; and with him is pure light. And of the wisdom of God it is written: that she knows the **outcomes of times and ages** <note>original: "eventus temporum & Seculorum"</note>. 
<margin>
Wisdom 8:8.
</margin>
Therefore the wise man <note>Referring to King Solomon, traditionally considered the author of the Book of Wisdom</note> also boasts that 
<margin>
Wisdom 7:17.
</margin>
God has given him the knowledge of all things; that he knows not only how the world is made, and the powers of the elements, but also the beginnings, ends, and middles of time.

<margin>4 Esdras 14:5 <note>Also known as 2 Esdras in the English Apocrypha.</note></margin>
And God the Lord boasts and relates to *Ezra* how he showed his servant *Moses* on Mount *Sinai*, alongside other secrets, also the **secrets of times** <note>original: "Secreta temporum"</note>, that is the mysteries and the end of the times.

From all of which so much is to be understood: that God the Lord alone truly knows the distinction of the **ages** <note>original: "Seculorum"</note> and times; and thereafter those who occupy themselves in the **study** <note>original: "Studio"</note> of Divine Wisdom obtain from God, through his Divine Wisdom, the true knowledge of such things. But the others, who are outside of God, his Word, and heavenly wisdom, and thus wish to write or speak of such things through human wit, through **astrology** <note>original: "Astrologiam"</note>, or from human writings, they do so without any foundation; they grope and lose their way in the dark to such an extent that they can never arrive at any certainty. Therefore *Sirach* <note>Referencing the Book of Sirach, or Ecclesiasticus.</note> has rightly and well said:

<margin>Sirach 34:5.</margin>
> One's own divination and interpretation and dreams are nothing, and yet they cause one heavy thoughts: And where it does not come through the inspiration of the Most High, then think nothing of it.

## Chapter II.

->How many *ages* <note>original: "Secula"</note> or principal periods there are.<-

Since there is therefore no doubt that God the Lord has ordained certain distinctions of times—how and when each of them should begin and also end once more—many people have been found who have reflected upon these matters with great diligence and have made their division of the *ages* <note>original: "Seculorum"</note> or times. Thus *Augustine* <note>St. Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), a foundational Christian theologian who developed the "Six Ages of the World" theory.</note> has *constituted* <note>original: "constituiret"</note> sevenfold *ages* <note>original: "ætates"</note> or eras of the world, modeled after the six days of Creation and the seventh, as the Sabbath or day of rest; likewise according to the seven ages of man. He does so in such a way that he considers the first age to be that which existed in Adam's time, calling it *Infancy* <note>original: "Infantiam"</note>. The second, in the times of Noah, he calls *Childhood* <note>original: "Pueritiam"</note>. The third, in the times of Abraham, he calls *Adolescence* <note>original: "Adolescentiam"</note>. The fourth, in the times of David, he calls *Youth* <note>original: "Juventutem"</note>. The fifth, at the time of the Babylonian Captivity, he calls the *Decline from youth to old age* <note>original: "Declinationem à Juventute ad senectutem"</note>. The sixth, at the time when Christ came in the flesh, he calls *Old Age* <note>original: "Senectutem"</note>. The seventh, when Christ

Some years ago, a scholar wrote a treatise which he titled *On the Seven Judgments of the World* <note>original: "De Septem mundi judicijs"</note>. In this work, he divides the *ages* <note>original: "Secula"</note> or eras into seven types of God’s judgments. He indicates that the first judgment occurred when Adam and Eve were driven out of Paradise. The second, when the entire world was destroyed by the Great Flood. The third, when the people of Israel were led out of Egypt. The fourth, when the people of Israel were released from the Babylonian Captivity. The fifth, when Christ suffered and died. The sixth judgment is being held in this, our present time, until the approaching Day of the Lord. The seventh and last will be when Christ returns to judge the living and the dead.

Another man named *Carolus Gallus* <note>Charles de Galles (1530–1606), a theologian who applied the structures of the Book of Revelation to historical periods.</note> described a *New Prophetic Key to the Apocalypse of Saint John* <note>original: "novam Clavem Propheticam Apocalypseos Johannis"</note> a few years ago. In this, he identifies seven distinct *ages* <note>original: "Ætates"</note> or periods, modeled first after the seven lampstands—or the seven letters sent to the leaders of the churches in Asia. Secondly, according to the seven seals which the Lamb opened. Thirdly, according to the seven trumpets which were blown in succession. And fourthly, according to the seven golden bowls full of the wrath of God, poured out by the seven angels. He explains how these seven distinct periods have proceeded from the time of the Apostles until the

## The II. Chapter.

### How many Eras or Principal Times there are.

BEcause there is no doubt that God the Lord has ordained certain divisions of time; how and when each should begin and also end again: Many people have therefore been found who have considered these matters with great diligence and made their own division of the *Ages* <note>original Latin: "Seculorum"</note> or times. Thus, *Augustine* <note>Saint Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), a foundational Christian theologian whose "Six Ages of the World" theory linked biblical history to the six days of creation.</note> has established seven types of *ages* <note>original Latin: "ætates"</note> or stages of the world, modeled after the six days of Creation and the seventh as the Sabbath or day of rest; and also according to the seven stages of human life. He does this in such a way that he considers the first age to be that which existed in Adam’s time, and calls it *Infancy* <note>original Latin: "Infantiam"</note>. The second, in the time of *Noah*, he calls *Childhood* <note>original Latin: "Pueritiam"</note>. The third, in the time of *Abraham*, he calls *Adolescence* <note>original Latin: "Adolescentiam"</note>. The fourth, in the time of *David*, he calls *Youth* <note>original Latin: "Juventutem"</note>. The fifth, at the time of the Babylonian Captivity, he calls the *Decline from youth to old age* <note>original Latin: "Declinationem à Juventute ad senectutem"</note>. The sixth, at the time when Christ came in the flesh, he calls *Old Age* <note>original Latin: "Senectutem"</note>. The seventh, when Christ

## Chapter VI.

### Whether this teaching of the future golden age is an entirely new teaching?

So that no one may think that what we mentioned in the previous chapter regarding a still-remaining golden age is completely and entirely new and never heard of before, we cannot avoid reminding and pointing out here: that beyond this—which is solidly grounded in **Prophetic and Apostolic scriptures** and lies hidden within them like a magnificent treasure—the most prominent and oldest **Christian Church Fathers** <note>original German: *Altvätter*, the influential early theologians of the Christian church</note> from the time of the Apostles onward were also of this opinion: that after this time, another time or **Age** <note>original Latin: *Seculum*</note> would come and begin: In which time, according to the eternal truth of God, the prophetic promises **concerning the restoration of Israel** <note>original Latin: *de restitutione Israelis*, the theological concept of Israel returning to God’s favor</note> must for the most part still be fulfilled. Which time they determined to be **one thousand years**, following the testimony of the **Revelation of John**: Therefore, they were called **Chiliasts** <term>Chiliastæ: from the Greek word *chilioi* (thousand), those who believe Christ will reign on earth for a millennium</term> or **Millenarians** <term>Millenarij: from the Latin word *mille* (thousand), an alternative name for Chiliasts</term> by others who did not wish to agree with this opinion.

<summary>The author argues that the "last days" mentioned in the New Testament referred to the transition from the Old Testament era to the present, and introduces the historical belief in a future thousand-year "golden age" held by the early Church Fathers.</summary>
<keywords>last hour, golden age, Church Fathers, restoration of Israel, Millennium, Chiliasts, Millenarians, Revelation of John</keywords>

## Chapter VII.
### That one Main Epoch hangs upon another.

<margin>
Genesis 1.
</margin>
<margin>
4 Ezra 6, 8, 9, 10.
</margin>
Just as God the Lord, in the beginning of His creation, distinguished between day and night, light and darkness—so that the evening is an end of the day and a beginning of the following night, and again the morning is an end of the night and a beginning of the following day—even so has Almighty God linked the three main epochs <term>Hauptzeiten: major historical or cosmic periods</term> together, yet properly distinguished them from one another; so that the end of one main epoch is simultaneously a beginning of the next following epoch. As was clearly revealed to the priest Ezra <note>original: *Esdra*, referring to the apocryphal book of 4 Ezra</note> in a vision and explained through a fine parable. For, as he was concerned with such a distinction of the times and desired to know how one time would follow upon another, God gives to him through

Just as Jacob followed Esau when he was born, and held him by the hand at his heel.

That such a thing has now happened and come to pass with the end of the first main epoch <term>Hauptzeit: a major period of history or cosmic time</term> and the beginning of the second main epoch, is to be seen in this: that when the second age of the **New Testament** began with **Christ** and His ministry <note>original: *Ampte*</note> on earth, it was at that same moment the decline and the end of the first age of the **Old Testament**. At that time, the glorious city of **Jerusalem** was destroyed, and through this, all **Jewish jurisdiction** <note>original: *Iurisdiction*</note>, along with the sacrifices and the previously customary Jewish worship, was completely and utterly abolished. And immediately thereafter, both here and there across the world, the **Christian religion** properly began, as did the **Roman Monarchy** <note>original: *Römische Monarchia*</note> in the worldly government.

<margin>

In the same manner, the future golden and final age, or century <term>Seculum: an era or a long period of time</term>, hangs upon this second main epoch of the New Testament which still continues today; it follows immediately upon it, and its beginning will be the end of this, our present time. This is indicated shortly, yet clearly enough, in the book of **Ezra**, where the angel in a vision says to him: The **Day of Judgment** shall be the end of this time, and the beginning of the future immortality, when perishable existence <note>original: *verrückliche wesen*</note> shall cease.

Now, the reader must take good note throughout this entire treatise <term>Tractatu: a formal written discourse or book</term> of how one age hangs upon another and follows directly after it, such that the end of one major epoch is the beginning of the next; in this way, he may read the same with all the better understanding and benefit.

# The VIII. Chapter.
## What existed and occurred before the beginning of the first Major Epoch.

<margin>Origen, in the 3rd book of *Principles* <note>original: *Orig. in 3 lib. per.* This refers to the theologian Origen's work *De Principiis*.</note></margin>The ancient Church teacher Origen committed a great error in this matter, for he wrote and believed that just as another world would follow and begin after this current world (according to Holy Scripture), so too had there been many other worlds before this present one, one after another. The pagan philosophers Democritus and Epicurus likewise wrote such things. However, if we look through Holy Scripture, we find explicitly how this world and all created things had their certain <margin>Genesis 6 <note>Though the margin reads *Gen. 6*, the context suggests a reference to the creation narrative in Genesis 1 or perhaps the limitations God placed on time.</note></margin>beginning. In this beginning, God the Lord ordained <margin>Isaiah 43, v. 10, 13 <note>original: *Esai. 43. v. 10. 13.*</note></margin>days, time, and hours, of which none had ever existed before. Therefore, God says through the prophet Isaiah: "Before me no God was formed, nor will there be any after me; I am he, even before there ever was a day." <note>The author provides a vernacular rendering of Isaiah 43:10-13.</note> Just as there was no time before the beginning of these three ordained major epochs <term>Hauptzeiten: the primary divisions of history, often understood here as the Age of the Law, the Age of the Gospel, and a future Age of Spirit</term>, so also <margin>Revelation 10:6 <note>original: *Apo, 10.6*</note></margin>the angel in the Revelation of John <note>original: *Offenbarung Iohannis*</note> swears a great oath that after the completion of such ages, there shall be no more time thereafter.

<summary>This page introduces Chapter 8, refuting the idea of multiple worlds before or after the current one as proposed by Origen and pagan philosophers, asserting instead that time has a definite beginning and end ordained by God.</summary>
<keywords>epochs, Origen, creation, Isaiah, Revelation, Democritus, Epicurus, theology of time</keywords>

# Chapter IX.
## Concerning the wondrous things that occurred after the beginning of the first Major Epoch.

<margin>4 Esdras 9:5-6 <note>A reference to the apocryphal book of 2 Esdras, often called 4 Esdras in older German bibles.</note></margin>The Angel Uriel says to the priest Ezra in a vision, among other things: just as everything made in the world has a beginning and an end, and its end is manifest; so also the time of the Most High has a manifest beginning with wonders and powers, and ends with deeds and signs. From this indication it clearly appears: that it is the same with the three previously mentioned major epochs <term>Hauptzeiten: The author's term for the three primary eras of world history</term> ordained by God; that each of them begins with wonders and signs, and also ends with wonders and signs. So that we may truly learn whether it has hitherto occurred and come to pass in this way with the beginning and end of the first epoch, as well as with the beginning and the approaching end of the second epoch of the New Testament—in which we still remain—we shall take up not only biblical but also secular histories and examine how the matter truly stands.

Now, concerning the beginning of the first major epoch, namely when the world had its beginning: exceedingly great and such miraculous works occurred then, into which, even to this day, even the most highly wise among the pagan philosophers <note>original: *Philosophis*</note> cannot orient themselves or fathom by speculation. For since previously nothing physical <note>original: *Cörperliches*</note>

Thus, the almighty Creator most faithfully and diligently warned Man (whom He had created in His image, loved so dearly, appointed as ruler and governor of the entire earth and all other creatures, and placed within this magnificent garden of Paradise) against such a Tree of Death, and expressly forbade him from eating of it. ...that she had been falsely persuaded and seduced by the same <note>Referring to the serpent mentioned on the previous page.</note>.

Following this, a great enmity arose between the woman and the serpent; also, a very great and heavy punishment was imposed upon each of them by **God**: The ground was cursed for the sake of mankind, so that henceforth, if left uncultivated, it should bear nothing but thorns and thistles. Also, **Adam** and **Eve** were driven out of **Paradise**, and all opportunities were cut off from them so that they could no longer enter it. They thus lost a great part of the authority which they previously (as rulers of the whole world) had held over all creatures, even the most cruel and fierce animals; and thus they now had to fear these same creatures and be on their guard against them. To all of this was added this very great misfortune: that of the two sons, **Cain** and **Abel**, whom **Adam** fathered with **Eve**, one struck the other dead. All of these things were, after the beginning of the <term>first main era</term> <note>Original: *Hauptzeit*. In early modern historiography, this refers to the first of the great epochs of world history, spanning from the Creation to the Great Flood.</note>, such great "miracles" <note>The author continues to use the term *Wunderwercke* (miracles/wonders) to describe these world-altering, shocking events, emphasizing how they fundamentally changed the nature of reality.</note>; so that (so to speak) no angel in heaven, nor anyone else, could have previously imagined that anything of the kind should happen or take place. Just as (aside from this <term>fratricide</term> <note>Original: *Bruder Mordt*.</note>) since then, for as long as this world has existed, no such "miracle" has occurred.

<summary>This page describes the immediate consequences of the Fall, including the cursing of the earth, the expulsion from Paradise, the loss of human dominion over animals, and the first murder. The author characterizes these tragic shifts in the cosmic order as "miracles" because of their unprecedented and shocking nature.</summary>

## Chapter 10.

### Concerning wonderful things that happened and occurred in general, one after another, toward the end of the <term>first main era</term> <note>Original: *Hauptzeit*. While the previous page associated this term with the era from Creation to the Flood, the author here uses it to denote the era ending with the coming of Christ.</note> up until the beginning of the second main era—that is, around one hundred years before the birth of **Christ**.

<margin>Matthäus Zeisig. Johannes Praetorius, *On Comets*.</margin>
Before the birth of **Christ**, and thus toward the end of the first main era, several large comets appeared: one in the year 91 BC; one in the year 84; one in the year 60; one in the year 41; one in the year 29; and one in the year 22 BC. Indeed, around that same time, so many and wonderfully terrifying comets appeared as had never been heard of before: As the poet **Virgil**, who lived at that time, indicates through this verse:

<margin>Book 1 of the *Georgics*, verse 488.</margin>
> Never at any other time did so many heavenly comets shine. <note>original: "Non aliàs divos toties fulsisse Cometas." Virgil wrote this in his *Georgics* to describe the celestial omens following the assassination of Julius Caesar.</note>

<margin>Julius Obsequens, *On Prodigies*, Book 5.</margin>
Among the people of Arezzo <note>original: *Aretinern*. Referring to the residents of Arretium, an ancient city in Tuscany.</note>, around the year 93 BC, this wonder occurred: when bread was broken open, red blood flowed out of it. Also, around the same time, it hailed for seven whole days in a row.

<margin>Lycosthenes, *On Prodigies and Omens*.</margin>
In the year 89, a woman gave birth to an elephant. <note>In historical chronicles of this period, "giving birth to an elephant" was a common way to describe a severely deformed fetus that appeared "monstrous" to observers.</note> In the year 88 BC, all tame animals—such as dogs, cats, cattle, and horses—fled from people, out of the villages and towns, and ran into the forests and wilderness with great crying.

## The Eleventh Chapter.

->Concerning the earthquakes that were observed within a hundred years before the end of the first main epoch, up to the beginning of the second main epoch, and thus until the birth of Christ.<-

Earthquakes are, for reasons we intend to point out below in Chapter 18, not unjustly regarded as extraordinary and terrifying wonders of nature; for this reason, we shall touch upon them here in a specific chapter. 
<margin>Orosius</margin> <note>Paulus Orosius (c. 375–418 AD) was a Christian historian whose works were often used to correlate natural disasters with divine providence.</note>
Within some eighty-odd years before the birth of Christ, through the entire Orient and Syria, as well as in Italy and other lands, there was such a terrible earthquake that a great many castles, houses, and buildings—both in the cities and in the countryside—collapsed to the ground. In Syria alone, some seven thousand people were killed. Likewise, around Mutina <note>The ancient Roman name for the city of Modena in Italy.</note>, two mountains burst apart, fell against each other, and crushed many animals and people. 
<margin>Florus. Plutarch. Orosius. Augustine. Fall. On Earthquakes.</margin> <note>original: *de terræ motibus*</note>
In the 70th year before the birth of Christ, a great and cruel earthquake occurred in Asia <note>In this historical context, "Asia" refers to the Roman province of Asia Minor, modern-day Turkey.</note>. In the 67th year, there was such a terrible earthquake in Syria that cities and mountains collapsed into heaps, from which over 10,000 people were buried and killed. 
<margin>Augustine. Fales. 3 Plutarch.</margin>
In the 60th year before the birth of Christ, once again a terrible earthquake arose in Syria as well as in Pontus <note>A region on the southern coast of the Black Sea.</note> and elsewhere, by which many mountains and stone cliffs were split apart, many cities fell to ruin, and many thousands of people miser-

## Chapter XIII

### Concerning the notable, manifold, and quite wondrous changes that occurred within a hundred years—from the end of the first main epoch <note>The author uses "Hauptzeit" to divide history into major eras; here, the transition from the Hellenistic/Republican era to the Roman Empire.</note> to the beginning of the second main epoch, or until the birth of Christ—within governments in general, in entire kingdoms, principalities, and lands.

The changes that tend to follow one another quickly and frequently in governments <term>Governments (Regimenter: referring to the ruling structures or regimes of the time)</term> are not the least or smallest wonders that occur in the world; through them, a general change in the government of the whole world is especially indicated and predicted beforehand, as it were. <sig>E v</sig>

...had the city of Rome set on fire in twelve places, intentionally and out of pure wantonness <note>original: "Vorwiß," implying a reckless or meddlesome curiosity</note>, in the 65th year after the birth of Christ. He allowed it to burn for six full days and seven nights together, and thereby permitted around eighty thousand houses—along with great wealth and many people—to burn and perish. All this was done simply because it came into his mind to see what a great fire there must have been 1,240 years earlier when the city of Troy was burned down.
<margin>Eusebius and Cornelius Tacitus, Book 15.</margin>

Now we will briefly recount the notable and great miracles <term>prodigies|supernatural omens or signs believed to foretell significant events</term> that occurred before the destruction of the city of Jerusalem, and how miserably the first major era subsequently ended. Seven years before the destruction of Jerusalem, a blood-red comet appeared. In that very same year, a poor Jewish man named Jesus, son of Ananias <note>original: "Jesus, genant Ananj"</note>, a peasant's son, began his lamentation over Jerusalem. He constantly cried "Woe!" over the city and allowed neither beatings, torture, nor threats to dissuade him from it.
<margin>Cornelius, cited above. Josephus, *The Jewish War*, Book 6, Chapter 1; and Book 5, Chapter 14.</margin>

In the year immediately preceding the destruction of Jerusalem, for almost the entire year, a comet appeared over the Temple in the shape of a fiery sword. And during the Easter holidays <note>Though the text uses "Osterlichen" (Easter), in this historical context it refers to the Jewish festival of Passover.</note>, and every night in succession around the ninth hour, the Temple and its altar shone with such light as if it were day. Also, when a cow standing before the altar was about to be slaughtered, she gave birth to a lamb in the middle of the Temple. *Pliny* also writes that at about the same time (which our people believe happened around the birth of Christ, or shortly thereafter), a bright comet was seen toward the north for seven consecutive days, always at eleven o'clock. It grew to such an extent that one could not look at it, and a human image is said to have been seen within it. The heathens interpreted this comet as referring to the Emperor *Augustus*. Others describe this miracle thus: that a very great light was seen, and in it the figure of a small child. When the heathen artists <note>original: "Künstler." Here referring to skilled scholars, magi, or those practiced in the interpretation of signs.</note> were asked about this wonder, they are said to have answered: This child, who was prefigured by this sign, would be *Augustior* <note>A Latin comparative meaning "more majestic" or "more venerable" than Augustus himself.</note> than *Augustus*. He would also be worthy of being worshipped by Augustus himself.

Beyond this, in the very year that Christ was born, good clear oil flowed out of a spring in *Rome* for an entire day. Others also write that at the time of Christ’s birth, several springs were said to have had the taste of wine; therefore *Fabricius* <note>Georg Fabricius (1516–1571), a German Protestant poet and scholar known for his sacred Latin poetry.</note> composed these verses:

> Balsams flow with oil, the tree sweats;
> In the rivers there is the flavor of wine.

Joseph, her betrothed, when he found that his bride Mary was pregnant—because he knew himself to be innocent of such a deed and therefore initially took this for a common occurrence and human work, just as any other woman is accustomed to becoming pregnant—did not want to tolerate this state of affairs, but was willing to secretly leave his bride and go away from her. However, an angel appeared to him in a dream and revealed to him the nature of Mary’s pregnancy, and what a great work was to arise and take its beginning from it. 

As for the priest Zechariah: the angel Gabriel also appeared to him in the temple and revealed to him the beginning of the second *seculum* <note>original: "Seculi." Latin for an age, century, or era. [Of the] estates that remained, one part has been seized and **secularized** <note>original: "weltlich gemacht," referring to the process of transferring property from the Church to worldly or state control</note>; while on the other hand, where one could not easily do so for the sake of honor, the monasteries and other ecclesiastical persons (as has happened in the Crown of France and in several places in German lands) have nonetheless been partly drained by excessive borrowing of money, or otherwise **burdened** <note>original Latin: "prægrivirt"</note> and sucked dry through taxes and **collections** <note>original Latin: "Collectis"</note> in many ways.

This is a new and previously unheard-of thing; for it is fundamentally true and certain that if one were to add up all the religious property and ecclesiastical estates that have been taken from the Church and the clergy since the time of the Apostles, it would not amount to nearly as much as that which has been withdrawn from the Roman Church and its affiliates—in terms of entire <term>archdioceses</term> <note>the highest-ranking districts of the church, led by archbishops</note>, bishoprics, many monasteries, religious estates, rents, and income—within living memory alone, taken from several kingdoms, principalities, lands, and cities, and for the most part secularized.

## The Eighteenth Chapter.

### Regarding Many Earthquakes, which have been felt in many different places over these 100 years, from the Year of Christ 1500 until 1600.

<margin>Nahum 1:5–6</margin>
The Prophet Nahum speaks thus in his prophecy: The mountains quake before the Lord, and the hills melt; the earth trembles before him, as well as the world and all who dwell therein. Who can stand before his indignation? And who can abide in the fierceness of his anger? His fury is poured out like fire. For this reason, the present chapter deals with earthquakes, so that one may understand from them that earthquakes are not only a punishment from God and proceed from Divine wrath, but also that they are such a severe punishment which tends to catch masters and servants, poor and rich, by surprise. For although one can perhaps rescue and preserve people in conflagrations <note>original: "Fewersbrunsten"; refers to large, destructive fires</note>, and at times come to the aid of the afflicted in great floods, times of war, pestilence, famine, and other similar punishments of the land and the home; yet it is quite a different matter with earthquakes. This is because they often occur so suddenly and unexpectedly that everything collapses into a heap <note>original: "alles über einen Hauffen fällt"</note>, against which no salvation may be found through art and skill, speed, might, power, or wealth. For, although one [until]—

# Chapter XVIII.

## Concerning many earthquakes that have been felt in many different places over these 100 years, from the Year of Christ 1500 until 1600.

<margin>Nahum 1:5–6</margin>
The prophet Nahum <note>a minor prophet in the Old Testament whose book concerns the judgment of God</note> speaks thus in his prophecy: The mountains quake before the Lord, and the hills melt, the earth heaves before him, as well as the world and all who dwell therein. Who can stand before his indignation? And who can abide in his fierce anger? His wrath burns like fire. Therefore, this present chapter deals with earthquakes, so that one may understand from them that earthquakes are not only a punishment from God and originate from Divine wrath, but that they are also such a severe punishment that they tend to overtake both masters and servants, the poor and the rich alike. 

For although people can perhaps be saved and preserved in conflagrations <note>large, destructive fires</note>, and sometimes one can come to the aid of the afflicted in great floods, times of war, pestilence, famine, and other such punishments of the land and home; yet it is a completely different matter with earthquakes. This is because they often occur so suddenly and unexpectedly that everything collapses into a heap, against which no rescue may be found through art and skill, speed, power, force, or wealth. For, although one might even—

while beneath the collapsed houses and buildings, people are still heard roaring, howling, screaming, and lamenting miserably: yet one often cannot come to their aid at all; instead, one must let them die and perish pitifully in their unspeakable misery and pain. Thus, without any doubt, earthquakes are the most serious and burdensome punishments from God that one can ever imagine. When we now set before our eyes how many terrible, large earthquakes have arisen here and there for some time: we should rightly reflect that such things are not ordained by God the Lord without special cause. In particular, however, we should be wary and consider it a matter of great importance that in these last 100 years alone, more earthquakes have arisen than were recorded in the 300 years prior to them.

## Chapter 19

## Chapter XIX.

->About many terrible uprisings and<-
->highly dangerous revolts of the<-
->subjects against their authorities, which<-
->arose in entire kingdoms and lands<-
->over these past hundred years, from<-
->the year 1500 until 1600.<-

If one wished to describe each and every uprising and revolt that has occurred in every single city over these 100 years, one would need an entire book for that purpose. Therefore, in this place, we wish to briefly recount only those uprisings that have taken place in entire kingdoms and lands, and in some of the most prominent capital cities, during these 100 years.

## Chapter XIX.
### Concerning many gruesome riots and very dangerous rebellions of subjects against their authorities, which have arisen in entire kingdoms and lands during these hundred years, from the year 1500 to 1600.

If one were to describe each and every one of those riots and rebellions that have occurred in every single city during these 100 years, one would need an entire book for that purpose. Therefore, at this place, we wish only to briefly recount those riots that have occurred in entire kingdoms and lands, and in some of the most prominent capital cities, during these 100 years.

One should, however, take special note of this: it certainly did not happen without particular, thought-provoking causes. Just as in the hundred years before the **birth of Christ**, the most numerous uprisings arose against the **Romans**, so too in these past hundred years—aside from the **Indian** uprisings <note>This refers to the indigenous revolts in the Americas following Spanish and Portuguese colonization.</note>—most revolts and uprisings occurred (if not always in **Constantinople**, where the **Turkish Emperor** <note>The Sultan of the Ottoman Empire.</note> holds court, then elsewhere in **Turkey** and against the Turkish Emperor). There were twenty-seven of these in various places and times.

## Chapter XX.

This chapter primarily concerns great lords and **potentates** <note>original: "Potentaten"; powerful rulers or monarchs.</note>, of whom the prophet **Isaiah** <note>original: "Esaias"; the author is quoting Isaiah 40:23-24.</note> says: that God brings princes to nothing, as if their stem had neither plant, nor seed, nor root in the earth; so that when a wind blows upon them, they wither, and a whirlwind carries them away like stubble—

<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous chapter regarding the divine judgment and worldly instability of the 16th century, specifically focusing on the tragic ends of European monarchs.</meta>

(144)

...leads. Therefore, great lords may see themselves reflected in this: how God has managed His household so wondrously with those of their kind over these past 100 years. He has not only allowed so many different kingdoms and lands to pass into other hands, but has also caused many kings and princes to lose their lives in sudden and violent ways, while letting others fall into a heavy and burdensome debt, and some even into the most extreme poverty and ruin.

Initially, we wish to give an account of the royal, princely, and other persons of high standing who have lost their lives in a violent or treacherous manner during these 100 years. Thus, first of all, several Popes in Rome were dispatched with poison; such as **Alexander VI**, **Leo X**, **Hadrian** <note>original: "Adrianus"; refers to Pope Adrian VI.</note>, and **Clement VII**. **Francis** <note>original: "Franciſcus"; Francis III, Duke of Brittany, the eldest son of King Francis I of France.</note>, the son of King **Francis** of France, was poisoned by an Italian. King **Henry II** <note>original: "Henricus II"</note> of France was so badly injured in a tournament that he died from it. His son, **Charles IX** <note>original: "Carolus IX"</note>, died of poison <note>While rumored at the time to be poisoned, modern historians often attribute his death to tuberculosis.</note>. The second son, King **Henry III** <note>original: "Henricus III"</note>, was shamefully and treacherously stabbed to death by a monk <note>Jacques Clément, who assassinated the King in 1589.</note>. The third son, **Francis**, Duke of Alençon <note>original: "Herzog von Valenzen"; Francis, Duke of Anjou and Alençon.</note>, had been poisoned several years earlier. Their mother, Queen **Catherine de' Medici** <note>original: "Catharina Medices"</note>, was (as it is believed) executed with poison by her own aforementioned son, King **Henry III**. The old King of **Navarre**, **Antoine** <note>original: "Antonius"; Antoine de Bourbon.</note>, was shot during the siege of **Rome** <note>The text likely means the Siege of Rouen (1562), where Antoine was actually killed, though the OCR reads "Rom".</note>. --- Page 184 ---
<meta>This page transitions from specific dynastic successions in the late 1590s to a broader historical overview of the territorial losses suffered by the Roman Catholic Church during the 16th century, specifically through the secularization of ecclesiastical lands across Northern and Central Europe.</meta>

(155)

<margin>
Chytraeus, Book 11; and Dresser, in *The Sixth Millennium*. <note>References to works by historians David Chytraeus (1530–1600) and Matthäus Dresser (1536–1607).</note>
</margin>
As long as the Roman Church has stood, it has never previously lost as much of its property and income (if one should mention it) as has been taken from it during these last 100 years alone. For first, in the year 1525, the entire land of **Prussia** (which previously, for several hundred years, had been an ecclesiastical estate belonging to the **Teutonic Order**) was turned into a secular **Duchy**. Afterwards, at various times and one after another, entire bishoprics, abbeys, and monasteries were gradually withdrawn from the Papacy and transformed into secular estates; namely in **Saxony**, the **Archbishopric of Magdeburg** and the **Bishopric of Halberstadt**; in **Meissen**, the **Bishoprics of Merseburg**, **Naumburg**, and **Meissen**. In the **March** <note>The Margraviate of Brandenburg</note>, the **Bishoprics of Brandenburg**, **Havelberg**, and **Lebus** <note>original: "Leuz"</note>; in **Westphalia** and **Lower Saxony**, the **Archbishopric of Bremen**, as well as the **Bishoprics of Minden**, **Osnabrück**, **Verden**, **Paderborn**, **Lübeck**, and **Ratzeburg**. In **Prussia**, two bishoprics, **Samland** <note>original: "Sambia"</note> and **Pomesania** <note>original: "Pomezon"</note>; in **Pomerania**, the **Bishoprics of Schwerin** and **Cammin**; in **Holstein**, the **Bishopric of Schleswig**. In the **Kingdom of England**, two archbishoprics and 20 bishoprics. In the **Kingdom of Denmark**, seven bishoprics; in the **Kingdom of Sweden**, several bishoprics; also **Courland**, which previously had likewise been an ecclesiastical territory and a distinguished house of the **Teutonic Order**, was in the year 1561

# Chapter 21

## Concerning various other quite wondrous things that have appeared in the 100 years up to now: And how these exactly correspond with the wondrous matters that occurred in the hundred years preceding the birth of Christ.

Up to this point, we have recounted what many and terrible earthquakes, uprisings, changes in governments <term>Regimenten</term> <note>Referring to systems of rule, administrations, or political orders</note>, and other strange things have occurred during these last 100 years; the likes of which have hardly been seen or heard of in any century <note>original: *Centenario*</note> since the world began, or at least since the birth of Christ. Now, if this (as I hope) will give the reader much cause for reflection—namely, that undoubtedly something special and unusual must follow from all of this—then such reflection will certainly be further strengthened here, and the reader confirmed in such an opinion, when I am able to present here certain things and prove from historical accounts <note>original: *Historien*</note> those events which have never happened since the world began, except in the 100 years before the birth of Christ, and yet have been noticed once again in the most recent hundred years, from the year 1500 to 1600. This is evident not only in several remarkable heavenly signs <note>These "signs" often referred to astronomical events like comets or eclipses viewed as omens</note> but also in earthly matters, and in how they correspond with one another on both sides. ## Chapter XXII.

### Why precisely these last hundred years have seen more wondrous things than before; also why such strange things have occurred, the likes of which have never before been heard of or experienced.

One finds many wicked and godless people who, in truth and certainty, believe neither in the Word of God nor in God Himself. Also in their hearts and minds (even if they pretend otherwise with their mouths), they cannot be persuaded to believe that there is yet another and better life beyond, and to be expected by us, than this current, base, and transitory life. To such people, God has wished to place before their eyes all kinds of wonders and signs <note>These "wonders and signs" likely refer to astronomical events, monstrous births, or other "prodigies" which Early Modern people interpreted as divine messages.</note> that occur beyond the ordinary course of nature. He does this so that He might—through such apparent, visible, and tangible means and indications—warn some of our crude and complacent <note>original: *sicheren* — In this context, "security" refers to *securitas carnalis*, a dangerous spiritual apathy or a false sense of safety where one no longer fears God's judgment.</note> godless crowd, drawing them away from their complacency and unbelief, and stir them toward faith in another and eternal life...

He then begins a theological defense of contemporary miracles, arguing they are divine wake-up calls for a godless and apathetic public.</summary>

## The XXIV Chapter.

### Whether one might also escape such impending, 
### indicated dangers, plagues, and 
### hardships.

From the account of what shall occur at the final 
waning <note>original: "Neige"; refers to the final decline or "dregs" of time as the world approaches its end.</note> of this present time and world, 
the Christian reader will have noticed the 
distinction: that just as during these past 
100 years, this misfortune, punishment, and 
plague occurred in one land or city, and 
in another something else, so too in the 
now-approaching waning of the 
world, different punishments, plagues, and 
persecutions will initially occur here and there 
over individual places; some 
notable lands and cities will feel and 
experience these especially before others; 
just as in France, the Netherlands, Hungary, and 
other places, they have unfortunately already 
experienced their share to a considerable 
extent, with great lamentation, damage, and 
the ruin of those so beautiful, magnificent 
lands; however, some of those who still sit 
prospereously <note>original: "in flore"; literally "in flower," meaning flourishing or in their prime.</note>, in peace, 
rest, joy, and in the highest glory, 
still have that to expect which 
we make mention of specifically <note>original: "specificè"</note> in 
our secret treatise <note>original: "Tractatu"</note> concerning 
the future third and golden age <note>The "Golden Age" or "Third Age" refers to a common 17th-century millenarian belief in a period of peace and spiritual renewal on Earth before the final Last Judgment.</note>; 
but toward the very end of the world, 
all danger, plague, persecution, and indeed 
final ruin... <meta>catchword: ...derbung (destruction/ruin)</meta>

For the Lord, the Lord of Hosts <term>Zebaoth: a Hebrew title for God meaning "of armies" or "of hosts," emphasizing His absolute power over all creation</term> is such that when He touches a land, it melts, so that all inhabitants must mourn; it shall completely overflow them like a flood, and be washed away as by the river in Egypt, etc.

However, with all the faithful, the godly, and the elect—and even more so with the children of divine wisdom <note>original: *Filiis Sapientiæ divinæ*</note>—it is a very different matter, as we can prove from the Old and New Testaments and confirm with stately examples: that such people have also physically escaped the evil and punishment decreed by God’s providence upon the land or city in which they lived.

For when God the Lord intended the devastation of the cities of Sodom and Gomorrah, He sent His

so that from those same places, when persecution began, many pious and God-fearing Christians escaped and settled in other and safer lands; whereby they nevertheless remained in peace and quiet, in body and life, and in the freedom of their religion. ## The XXVII Chapter.

### Why the true nature, condition, and circumstances of the coming third, golden, and final era have not long been known or revealed to the people: and thus shall and can be revealed no sooner than now, in the final days of this second era.

GOD the Lord has ordained it so that the secrets of each major era <term>Hauptzeit—literally "main time," referring to the distinct epochs of history in the author's prophetic timeline</term> must not be revealed until that same major era begins. For this is exactly how it happened with the mystery of the second major era and all that was attached to that same mystery: for although the entire teaching of the Gospel, or New Testament, was stately contained and grounded within the writings of the Old Testament, this mystery was nevertheless so hidden in said writings that neither the Rabbis nor their disciples, neither the scholars nor—much less—the laypeople, knew anything of a Gospel, of a servant’s form <note>original: *Knechtsgestalt*—referring to the humble, human appearance of Christ as a "suffering servant" rather than a royal conqueror</note> of the promised Messiah <note>original: *Messiæ*—Latin genitive form of Messiah</note>, of his suffering, death, and resurrection, of baptism, of the distribution <note>original: *Außspendung*—the ritual sharing of the elements in the Lord's Supper</note> of the body and blood of the promised Messiah, and many other such things belonging to the teaching of the New Testament Gospel; rather, each and every thing was entirely hidden from their eyes and understanding, until the second major era began, and in the—

<meta>catchword: same</meta>

The author draws a parallel to how the New Testament was hidden within the Old Testament until the time of Christ.</summary>

## Chapter XXVIII.

### Whether the very beginning of the third and golden age, when it finally arrives, will be known and manifest to many people; or whether it will be believed and noticed by many of them?

Above in Chapter 16, we explained that the beginning of this current second major era <term>Hauptzeit—the author's term for the distinct epochs of world history</term> of the New Testament was unknown and hidden from the whole world, except for a few individual persons; so much so that even the most prominent and learned among the Jews at that time knew less than nothing of such a change and shift of the ages, or of the approaching beginning of the...

<summary>The author argues that the details of the coming "Golden Age" remain a divine mystery that cannot yet be fully revealed, drawing parallels to the hidden transition between the Old and New Testaments.</summary>

--- Page 263 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from page 229, specifically finishing the sentence about the ignorance of the Jewish leadership at the dawn of the Christian era and drawing a parallel to the author's own time.</meta>

<page-num>230</page-num>

observed regarding the second Age <term>Seculi—referring here to the era of the New Testament</term>. Since this occurred back then, and because one must always weigh one age against the other and compare them in **corresponding matters** <note>original: *rebus convenientibus*</note>, one should not be surprised by our current circumstances. Even now, as a similar change of time is once again at hand, our own high priests and scribes <note>The author uses these biblical titles to critique the established church leaders and theologians of his own day</note> do not know about—or perhaps do not want to believe in—the beginning of this now-approaching **Golden Age**, and especially its true state and character.

For in the human writings they have used until now (just as the Jews used their **Talmud**), nothing fundamental or certain is understood regarding this matter. And even if it were to be presented to them through this, my current outline, it would nevertheless seem like a new and previously unheard-of thing to their ears. above all worldly honor, splendor, wealth, and
magnificence that might come to them. They
will also—no less than happened to the 
aforementioned persons at the beginning of the other <term>century</term> <note>original Latin: *Seculo*. Here, it refers to a Great Age or Era, specifically the "Second Age" or the Age of the Son (the New Testament era).</note>—
praise and glorify God for it with quite joyful
hearts and minds.

# The XXIX Chapter.
## Regarding the beginning of the third and golden age in general, and whether it is still far off, and what signs will occur therein.

In this entire little book, this is primarily
our <term>objective</term> <note>original Latin: *Institutum*.</note>: Because the
times and <term>ages</term> <note>original Latin: *Secula*.</note> begin and also end with
wonders and signs, we wish to <term>discuss</term> <note>original: *tractiren*.</note>
those miraculous signs of the three main ages
herein.

Now, we have thus far heard with
what signs and wonders the first age began
and ended; also what the initial wonder-works
of the current second main age <note>The "Second Age" traditionally refers to the period from the birth of Christ until the dawn of the final spiritual era.</note> were;
as well as what and which are the signs of
the end, some of which have occurred up to
this point, but some of which are still to be expected:

It therefore remains for us to also discuss in this chapter some of the signs and wonders which are to appear at the beginning of the approaching third—as the final—main age. Because these are mostly future things that have yet to occur, we cannot prove them through written histories and accounts, as we have done until now with past events. Instead, we must draw them from those places where future matters are prophesied and described: namely, from the Prophetic and Apostolic Scriptures, which also grandly proclaim to us that which is to happen in the future.

However, so that we might follow the natural order in this chapter by first speaking somewhat of past things, we cannot think otherwise—for various compelling reasons—than that this period is not yet the beginning itself, but rather a certain <term>vanguard</term> <note>original: *Vordrab*. A military term for the advance guard or scouts that precede the main army.</note> of the beginning of the future golden age. Namely, that within human memory, several extraordinary minds have arisen in all kinds of <term>sciences</term> <note>original Latin: *Scientiis*.</note> and <term>academic faculties</term> <note>original Latin: *Facultäten*.</note>, each of whom has brought something new to the stage in his respective field—something contrary to the previously customary ways—and defended it in public print.

Such as Dr. Martin Luther in <term>Theology</term> <note>original Latin: *Theologia*.</note>, who set himself quite earnestly against the entire Papacy and its religion, as it has been in vogue for a long time in this still-continuing <term>age</term> <note>original Latin: *Seculo*. In this context, it refers to the "Second Age" or the era of the church that the author believes is coming to an end.</note>. He preached publicly against it and wrote many books; his teaching then [reached] entire kingdoms, principalities, lands...

...new ways to wonderfully **fortify** <term>fortificiren</term> **castles** <term>Castel</term> and **fortresses** <term>Festungen</term>. Where can one also say that in ancient times such magnificent and orderly **pleasure gardens** <term>Lustgarten</term> <note>Renaissance gardens were celebrated for their geometric precision, water features, and botanical diversity, often seen as "living museums."</note> were built, and adorned with so many different foreign, unknown plants, as well as with so many magnificent walkways, as have been arranged and built within living memory in Italy, France, and Germany? Indeed, to be brief, I will pass over many other (See A at the end of the chapter <note>original: "Vide A sub finem Cap."</note>) ingenious **mechanical things** <term>Mechanischen Sachen</term>. 

Thus I must also mention those so artistic **clockworks** <term>Uhrwercken</term>, which were also only invented within living memory; and especially those so very small and delicate little watches that one is accustomed to wear around the neck, or to shape in many other fashions or ways; yes, what is more, such a very delicate and artistic little striking watch <note>A "striking" watch is one that uses a tiny hammer and bell to chime the hours, a feat of extreme miniaturization in the 16th century.</note> was once made within a finger ring: also, the course of the heavens and the stars has been modeled and made movable within a small clockwork. 

So too has one never before read or heard of large clockworks where such an artistic one was seen in any place as the clockwork in **Strasbourg** <term>Straßburg</term> <note>The astronomical clock of Strasbourg Cathedral was one of the wonders of the early modern world, featuring automated figures and complex celestial motions.</note> is; although six years ago in **Augsburg** <term>Augsburg</term> <note>Augsburg was a preeminent center for clockmaking and scientific instrument production in Europe.</note> at the house of a watchmaker's widow (whose husband passed away while working on that very clockwork) I saw a large clockwork which, in terms of ornament, many subtleties, various features, and artistry, surpasses the Strasbourg one, and is valued at many thousands of **guilders** <term>Gülden</term>; so that it is almost to be doubted whether its buyer, who...

<summary>This page discusses the superiority of modern advancements in military architecture, landscape gardening, and especially the miniaturization and complexity of mechanical clocks compared to antiquity.</summary>

(257)

...is the approaching beginning of the golden age: namely, that recently some (though very few) theologians are applying themselves to their sermons—setting aside all human glosses or their own interpretations—drawing them almost entirely ***word for word*** <note>original: *de verbo ad verbum*</note> from the Biblical scriptures, and arranging them in a certain order according to the subject matter at hand. Just as I myself have released a small prayer book in public print, I also intend in the future (if God grants me life) to describe a ***Theological Creed*** <note>original: *Symbolum Theologiam*</note> covering each and every article of faith and everything else which is necessary for us to know—not only for our **salvation** but also for each person in their station, life, and conduct—without any human addition, solely and alone from the Biblical scriptures. 

For such is a true ***Theology***, which can be held or criticized by no one as being **Popish** <note>A term used during the Reformation for the Roman Catholic Church.</note>, **Lutheran**, **Calvinist**, **Flaccian** <note>Followers of Matthias Flacius Illyricus, known for their strict Lutheran views on original sin.</note>, **Schwenkfeldian** <note>Followers of Caspar Schwenckfeld, who emphasized internal spiritual experience over external sacraments.</note>, **Anabaptist**, or otherwise **heretical**. Indeed, all the aforementioned religious adherents and sectarians cannot bypass it, but must allow such a ***Theology***—taken from the single and pure Word of God—to pass unchallenged and indisputable; unless they wished to be so very coarse and foolish as to publicly dare prefer their human teachings, ***Expositions***, ***Peculiarities***, and ***Opinions*** <note>original: *Expositiones*, *Singularitates* and *Opiniones*</note> over the written Word of God, whereby they would certainly [undermine] their teachings among their own listeners...

The sun emerges from the center <note>original: *Centro*</note> or ecliptic pole <note>original: *Polo Eclyptico*</note>, through which it revolves in its proper time. A drive of 11 spokes: the water wheel is 10 feet in diameter <note>original: *Diameter*</note>, rotates 16,200 times in 24 hours / and is driven by 3 cubic inches of water. Further information is provided by visual inspection <note>original: *Augenſchein*; literally "the appearance to the eye"</note>.
